NO restart, NO code/env/flag/state change.
**Root-cause-first (evidence before action):** journals (provider+AP) + direct on-chain `Jobs` map scan (py, decode via `ComputeMarketplace::Jobs`, key `blake2_128concat(u64 LE)`) + provider code + store. Fast-path flags `GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1` (9dcedb7) intact — untouched; HEAD `a251b79`, tree clean. Provider+AP `active`, `NRestarts=0`, both up since 11:20:52 (external batch); AP allocates REAL ids (#2040–#2046 match chain) and the provider heals Pending ⇒ read clients healthy (NOT the silent `client=None` class). Zero error/panic/fatal in `gpn-provider`/`gpn-accesspoint` since 12:00.
**Anomaly — #2042 `Running` wedge (genuine, new):** full-range chain scan (ids 0..2046): **2047 jobs {Completed 1727, Cancelled 293, Failed 25, Running 1, Pending 0}** ⇒ the lone non-terminal is **#2042**, `Running` since 13:04:59 and unchanged over ~7 min of re-checks. Off-chain it is finished: provider log `[job 2042] done — response 356 chars` at 13:05:01 and `llm_store.json` holds its payload. **ROOT CAUSE (verified in code):** the fast-path pipelined claim landed `accept`+`start` on-chain (state `Running`) but `submit_result_fast`/`complete_job_fast` never made it on-chain (the documented fast-path inclusion/nonce race, esc. #501). No path then heals it: `gpn_llm_provider.rs` reconcile handles only `Pending`+done, `AwaitingVerification`, and `Accepted` (lines ~479–560) — there is **no `Running` branch**; and step-1 (`:711`) skips any job whose off-chain `status=="done"`. Net: #2042 holds one `MaxJobsPerProvider` capacity slot until the item-9e janitor's deadline-gated `expire_job` reclaims it (bounded, not growing — same net class as the earlier `#1778`/`#1805` Running wedges, but those had NO store payload; #2042 HAS one). Fix = add a `Running`+done → `complete_job_fast` heal to reconcile (or drive any Running+done job to Completed) ⇒ touches provider code on the APPROVED fast path ⇒ needs MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE. No workaround applied.
**Anomaly 2 — e2e 8.9→48.1 s (>>5 s cap), ROOT-CAUSED to the poll cycle:** measured 8.9/15.2/28.6/29.8 (concurrent `/jobs` sweeps) then 48.1/29.2 s (clean, nothing else running). Provider claim timestamps show a **~28 s poll period** (`claiming LLM job #0` at 13:07:56 → 13:08:24 → 13:08:53 → 13:09:21 → 13:09:49 → 13:10:17, ~28 s apart). Root cause: `process_once` does the reconcile scan (128 ids × `get_job_state` = **3 `storage_at_head` reads each** ≈ 384 blocking RPCs) and, because the cycle (>10 s) always exceeds the `should_reconcile()` 10 s gate, it runs **every** pass ⇒ a newly created job waits up to a full ~28 s cycle to be claimed. (Not inference-bound: inference is ~1 s, e.g. #2045/#2046 claimed→done in 11 s incl. a 10 s claim→infer gap.) Storage reads are individually fast (raw `state_getStorage` 0.3 ms) ⇒ the cost is subxt per-call overhead × 384. This is the documented "AP request→claim latency" class (items a/d, esc. #501) now pinned to the reconcile window; fix = bound/cache the reconcile window or stop re-scanning ids with no store payload ⇒ provider code ⇒ MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE.
**Claims/orphans/txpool:** ~1 claim/request (distinct real ids #2041–#2046); the stale assignment entry `id "0"` (nonexistent on-chain) is re-claimed **once per cycle** (documented optimistic-claim residual, bound resets ⇒ wasteful txpool-only, no settlement harm); `assignments.json` = 1738 stale entries (documented). No claim storm, no Pending orphans.
**Action taken: NONE** (no hang/deadlock ⇒ no restart; both items are genuine but bounded and their fixes touch provider/AP code on/near the APPROVED fast path ⇒ MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE; no state wipe/workaround). Only this log file touched. STATUS: **GREEN** (functionally) — watch #2042: if Running-wedge count grows past 1 the slot leak is compounding.
## 2026-10-09 07:46 PT / 14:46 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — GENUINE ANOMALY (root-caused, self-recovered): gpn-explorer :8082 serves NOTHING for ~45 s+ after a single dashboard/`/api/jobs` load — single-threaded tiny_http server monopolised by an UNBOUNDED O(N) job scan (no cache, no per-request timeout); NO restart (not a deadlock — it drains itself), NO code/env/state change
**Root-cause-first (evidence before action):** journals + /proc + socket counters + source, then a bounded probe sequence. Fast path APPROVED-ON untouched (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1`); HEAD `3b89921`, tree clean; canonical WASM untouched. Node `gpn-fleet` healthy (mining #106643→#106644, 53 xts/block).
**Anomaly (GENUINE, functional):** all explorer routes returned **HTTP 000** (`/`, `/health`, `/api/blocks`, `/api/chain`, `/api/stats`, `/ping`) for >45 s, accepted conns piling in **CLOSE-WAIT**, yet the unit stayed `active` (so `Restart=on-failure` never fires and nothing is logged).
**DECISIVE EVIDENCE (this is NOT the 06:51 dead-ws class):** (1) explorer pid 2126282 held a **live ESTAB ws socket to :9944** (fd9) and was **actively churning it** — 150,597 B sent / 3 s, 20.7 MB total; (2) its **main/serve thread was in state `R`, burning ~93 % of a core** (utime +279 ticks/3 s); (3) only ~16 fds, the 8 CLOSE-WAIT all = this run's own probes (no external client). So the ws is alive and the process is stuck in a **hot RPC loop**, not silently defaulting.
**ROOT CAUSE (verified in `gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_explorer.rs`):** the server is **single-threaded** (`for request in server.incoming_requests() { route()… }`, tiny_http) and `jobs_panel()` runs an **unbounded scan** `for id in 0..next { get_job_state(id) + get_job_min_gflops(id) }` — **~3 storage reads per job, N≈1960+, no caching, no bound, no per-request timeout**. One `GET /` (dashboard → `jobs_panel`) or `GET /api/jobs` monopolises the only server thread for tens of seconds; every other route queues/`000`s until it drains. Not a deadlock (it terminates) and not a dead chain client — **self-inflicted scan saturation**, identical scaling class to the AP `/jobs` regression.
**Action taken: NONE (no restart):** re-probed after 45 s → `/health` 404 (0.0002 s), `/api/blocks` 200 (0.07 s), `/api/chain` 200 (0.05 s); main thread back to `S`, utime frozen ⇒ **the server drains itself**, so a restart would neither be justified (no deadlock) nor fix the cause (the O(N) scan).
**e2e (this run):** `POST :8092/v1/chat/completions` (llama3.2:1b) → real id **#1962**, answer `HELLO FROM GPN`, **wall 6.77 s** (>5 s cap = documented block-time-bound create-confirm class); provider claimed+served `#1962` (1 claim, no storm), reconciled to Completed. Provider+AP journal: **0 errors/panics/failed** in 20 min (only the benign fast-path heal-churn `healed leaked Pending #1961/#1962 → Completed`).
**Escalation (unchanged, needs MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE — code):** bound/batch/cache the explorer `jobs_panel` scan (and the AP `/jobs` sibling), and run the explorer HTTP server multi-threaded and/or under a per-request timeout, so a single dashboard load can't take the whole endpoint down. No workaround applied. STATUS: **GREEN** (explorer self-recovered; core e2e OK).

## 2026-10-09 06:51 PT / 13:51 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 1a4f19ab) — FIXED: gpn-explorer chain client dead (silent) → all storage reads defaulted; ONE targeted restart
**Root-cause-first (evidence reviewed before acting):** provider journal clean (no errors/panics); fast path APPROVED-ON untouched (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1`); all 10 gpn units active.
**SYMPTOM:** explorer :8082 `GET /api/jobs` = `{"jobs":[],"next_job_id":0}` while on-chain `ComputeMarketplace::NextJobId` = **1952** and the provider served #1946–#1951; `/api/account` balances 0.0; `/api/chain` difficulty pinned at the default **1e8**.
**ROOT CAUSE (evidence):** `ss -tnp` showed explorer pid 2061030 held **no TCP socket** to :9944 (provider/scheduler each held one). Every subxt storage read goes through `storage_at_head()` → `client.storage().at(hash).fetch()`; with the ws client dead each fetch silently returned `None` ⇒ 0/defaults, **while raw JSON-RPC endpoints (`/api/blocks`, head) still worked and masked it**. Unit stayed `active`, so `Restart=on-failure` never fired; no error was ever logged. Deployed binary (built Oct 8 10:02) has no reconnect/health logic ⇒ one dropped ws silently kills all storage-backed views until a manual restart.
**FIX (one, targeted at cause):** `systemctl --user restart gpn-explorer` → new pid 2126282 immediately re-established the ws socket (ESTAB fd9 → 127.0.0.1:9944).
**VERIFY:** `/api/jobs` now enumerates real jobs (Completed/Cancelled/Failed/Pending; only orphan = **Pending #1887**); `/api/chain` difficulty now live (**120,960,324** vs default 1e8); socket persistent; provider journal still clean; claims ~1/request (#1948–#1951 each claimed once, no storm).
**NOTES (report-only):** (1) explorer `/api/jobs` scan is slow (~40s+; one chain RPC per id, n≈1950) — same known scaling regression as the AP `/jobs` (bound/batch the scan ⇒ MAIN APPROVE). (2) e2e 500-char job: HTTP200 real answer but WALL **6.1–9.2s (>5s; known item h** block-time-bound create-confirm). (3) chain finality observation: `chain_getFinalizedHead` == **genesis** while head = 106467 (GRANDPA not advancing).
**ESCALATE (Jeremy):** add reconnect/health self-heal to the explorer's ChainClient — a single dropped websocket currently degrades the whole explorer to silent defaults with no log and no auto-recovery.

## 2026-10-08 02:55 PT / 09:55 UTC — PM07 driver (cron d496e0b0) — item **#10 Diffusion silent-wedge instrumentation** → **DONE** (live-verified)
**What changed (one file; no chain/genesis/runtime touched):** `gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_diffusion_provider.rs` — `run_job` used to `await` the diffusers helper via an unchecked `Command::output()`, so a hung render blocked the entire provider loop **indefinitely and silently** (the "silent wedge"; only a restart cleared it).  New `run_with_watchdog(cmd, timeout, label)`:
1. runs the helper under a **hard budget** (`GPN_DIFFUSE_TIMEOUT_SECS`; default **600 s** image / **1800 s** video);
2. emits a **30 s progress heartbeat** (`[watchdog] … still running — elapsed=Ns`) so a slow render is no longer dead air;
3. at the budget **kills the hung child** and logs `⚠ SILENT-WEDGE DETECTED` + marks the job errored off-chain (never silent);
4. a process-local `wedged` set **skips re-rendering a proven-wedge job** (avoids a tight GPU-burning retry loop); the chain-side expiry sweep reclaims it.
**Tests:** 5/5 bin tests green (success output; hung helper killed+flagged+prompt; nonzero-exit ≠ wedge; timeout defaults; wedged-marker roundtrip).
**Live-verified:** (1) real ImageGeneration `#1370` → heartbeat at 30 s → `done`, `/tmp/gpn_artifacts/job_1370.png`, on-chain **Completed**; (2) forced wedge (fake `sleep 300` script + `GPN_DIFFUSE_TIMEOUT_SECS=35` via a temporary systemd drop-in) → heartbeat at 30 s → `⚠ SILENT-WEDGE DETECTED — exceeded 35s — killed` → provider stayed `active` and kept polling; override removed + real script restored → `#1371` then rendered for real → on-chain **Completed** (no stranded job). Canonical genesis WASM `76f37b23…` intact; only `gpn_diffusion_provider` rebuilt/redeployed (`~/.local/bin`), `gpn-diffusion` restarted.
**Next:** item #11 (pallet distributions — staking + oracle rewards, real burn).

## 2026-10-07 19:42 PT / 02:42 UTC — PM07 driver (cron d496e0b0) — item **#5a Enable + live-verify the fine-tune provider** → **DONE**
**What changed (no chain/genesis/runtime touched):**
1. **`gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_finetune_provider.rs`** — added a **self-claim** step: there is no AP/scheduler routing path for `FineTuning` jobs, so the provider previously could never be triggered (it only *ran* jobs already `Running`+assigned). Now each pass it accepts + starts any recent (`CLAIM_WINDOW=64`) `Pending` finetune job that has an off-chain payload; `accept_job` stays gated on-chain by the min_gflops attestation check. Rebuilt release, installed `~/.local/bin/gpn_finetune_provider`.
2. **New systemd user unit `~/.config/systemd/user/gpn-finetune.service`** (mirrors gpn-diffusion; `Restart=always`) — enables the provider persistently. Uses a **dedicated provider wallet** (env `~/.gpn/finetune.env`, override of `PROVIDER_SEED`).
**Why the dedicated wallet (root cause):** running the finetune provider under the shared `provider.env` signer (same key as the LLM + diffusion providers) caused a **cross-process nonce race** — the busy LLM provider won nonce N+1, so the finetune provider's `complete_job` (fresh-nonce `submit_signed_call`) was dropped and the job wedged in `AwaitingVerification` (txpool empty, no error). Fixed by giving the service its **own registered/staked/attested wallet** `5Fh75o8NnKMdoLiW2n8m4g4jhjERm9NdcTdeTXLzCpkTdkDL` (drip 5000 GPN → `register_provider` → `deposit_stake` 1000 GPN → `submit_attestation` 30000 GFLOPS).
**Verified live (2026-10-07 19:38–19:39 PT):** created `POST /jobs` `FineTuning` (SmolLM2-135M), provider **self-claimed → LoRA-trained on the RTX 4060 → `submit_result` + `complete_job` all landed autonomously** ⇒ `ComputeMarketplace::Jobs[1184].state=Completed`, result_hash `bb54e209…`, store `status=done`, loss `1.935`; escrow paid the provider. Bonus: auto-merge (`gpn_servemodel.py`) produced an ollama model **`tuned-1184:latest`**. (`#1183` was the earlier run that proved the executor + exposed the nonce race; its manual `complete` closed it.) No runtime upgrade ⇒ no `set_code`, no rule-5a client restart needed.
**Next:** item #5b (exercise + verify video generation / ltx-video).

## 2026-10-07 17:52 PT / 00:52 UTC — PM07 driver (cron d496e0b0) — item **#3 Client-side RTT / user→nearest-node** → **DONE**
**What changed (smallest correct change; no chain/genesis touched):**
1. **New `gpn-agent/src/routing.rs`** — `RttOverrides` + `effective_latency_ms` (client-measured user→provider RTT wins over AP-measured, `0`/absent = fall back), `parse_rtt_overrides` (lenient; malformed/non-positive ignored, never fatal), `lookup_override` (matches by provider **address** or **status URL**, case-insensitive, `/status`+trailing-slash normalized). 5 unit tests green.
2. **`gpn_scheduler.rs`** — `pick()` now takes `&RttOverrides` and scores every branch (ready/puller/busy) by the effective **user-relevant** latency. Job requests (`POST /jobs`, `POST /route/{id}`, `POST /v1/chat/completions`) read an optional `{"rtt": {"<addr|url>": <ms>}}` hint. New unauthenticated **`GET /ping`** (client RTT probe / multi-AP selection); `/providers` now returns `address` so clients can key hints by address. 4 new bin tests (default pick = AP-nearest; client hint flips it; address- and URL-keyed; zero = unmeasured).
3. **New `gpn-agent/gpn_rtt.py`** — stdlib client helper: `measure_rtt`, `pick_nearest_ap`, `provider_rtts`, `ask` (attaches the hints; `GPN_APS`/`GPN_AP_TOKEN` env).
**Why:** the scheduler only ever minimised **AP→provider** RTT, so a user far from the access point paid that leg on every call regardless of the provider chosen (§6.1 gap). Now the *client* measures the path it actually uses and the AP prefers the provider nearest the **user**; behaviour is unchanged when no hints are sent (safe/back-compatible).
**Verified:** `cargo test --lib routing` 5/5, `cargo test --bin gpn_scheduler` 4/4. Built release, redeployed `~/.local/bin/gpn_scheduler`, `systemctl --user restart gpn-accesspoint`. **Live:** `/ping` → `{"ok":true,"ap":"0.0.0.0:8092"}`; `/providers` returns address `5ENqSp5Q…`; three e2e jobs (2 with RTT hints, 1 baseline) all `status:done`, `routed_to=5ENqSp5Q…`, `verify=sha256` OK. Multi-provider flip is unit-covered (only one healthy provider on the testnet today). No runtime upgrade ⇒ no `set_code`; no rule-5a client restart needed beyond reloading the AP binary.
**Next:** item #4 (Windows as an AI provider).

## 2026-10-07 14:52 PT / 21:52 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 1a4f19ab) — NEW ANOMALY: provider **#1102 heal-loop / false-success log storm** — ROOT CAUSE = **chronic tip fork** vs a remote AUTHORITY peer (23.241.109.255); reorgs revert #1102 completion ⇒ state flaps Pending↔Completed. No restart, no code change (network-level; escalate).
**Root-cause-first (evidence before action):**
1. `journalctl gpn-provider` since 14:40 = **only** `[provider] healed leaked Pending #1102 → Completed` every ~10–20s (19+ and counting, still going) — no other activity; txpool empty.
2. **The chain view itself flaps** (this is the cause, not the provider): direct `ComputeMarketplace::Jobs[1102]` reads via node RPC returned **byte-identical to #1100/#1101 (state=4 Completed, result_hash fde89a20… = the provider's own commitment)** at best #85976, but **state=0 Pending** at best #86002+, and a same-code probe (`get_job_state`) flipped **Completed↔Pending across successive reads** ⇒ the node's best block alternates between two forks.
3. `journalctl gpn-fleet` confirms the fork: `♻️ Reorg on #86009 → #86010, common ancestor #86008`; `🆕 Imported #86009` (a *different* hash than our mined 0x2ebb…); **29 reorgs since 14:00**; `finalized #0` (no finality gadget so tip state is never durable). Peer = `12D3KooWMXU21xpjF9NgG4NsYQo8EejaMCZ8JWPUbSW6q8T7d69Y` **roles=AUTHORITY best #86010**; ESTAB `192.168.1.20:30333 → 23.241.109.255:30333` (external).
4. Provider account is **healthy** (`0x664c…` == SS58 `5ENq…`: balance 1428.7 GPN, staked/attested, nonce **advancing 8241→8257** mid-check) ⇒ its heal txs *do* land — but only on **our** fork, which is immediately reorged away ⇒ #1102 never stays Completed canonically ⇒ reconcile re-heals forever.
**Mechanism / code weakness exposed:** the reconcile heal path (`gpn_llm_provider.rs:449`) uses **inclusion-free** `*_fast` submits and logs "healed …→Completed" on **broadcast Ok only** — it never verifies inclusion or dispatch, so under any non-landing condition (here: fork-reorg) it emits a permanent **false-success** line every cycle and makes **zero** progress.
**Fix: NONE applied** (no local hang/deadlock ⇒ restart is not indicated and would **not** stop the fork; code fix touches the APPROVED fast path ⇒ **MAIN APPROVE**). **Escalate to JEREMY:** (a) OPS — two producers are authoring testnet concurrently (local `--alice --force-authoring` node vs remote AUTHORITY 23.241.109.255): resolve the split (likely stale runtime spec 11↔12 after the aaa2200 c13edc2 upgrade, or a duplicate authority); until then tip state (recent job completions) keeps reverting. (b) CODE (needs MAIN APPROVE) — make reconcile verify inclusion/dispatch before logging healed, and skip/defer heal while the completion is reorg-flapping.
**Probe:** POST :8092 `/v1/chat/completions` — AP up (HTTP200 on /providers, provider healthy); e2e not re-run to a clean number because the fork makes it non-deterministic this window.
**State:** core services active; flags APPROVED-ON untouched; tree clean. STATUS: CORE GREEN, **testnet tip fork + #1102 heal-loop / NOT DONE**.

## 2026-10-07 12:34 PT / 19:34 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — NEW ANOMALY: STRANDED on-chain job **#1058** (escrow 512e9 locked, provider=null, NOT in AP/store) = **concurrent-create id-misattribution in the 0c5c6bb confirm logic**; NO restart, NO code change (fast path ⇒ needs MAIN APPROVE, unreachable)
**Root-cause-first:** unit state + provider/AP journals + direct chain reads + AP/provider stores reviewed BEFORE any action; fault-grep (panic/fatal/deadlock/ERROR/1010/1014) last 15m across gpn-provider/accesspoint/fleet = **0 hits**; only benign idempotent `healed leaked Pending` lines ⇒ **no restart**. Fast-path flags APPROVED-ON, intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` provider.env PID 1321305; `GPN_FAST_AP=1` ~/.gpn/accesspoint.env) — not reverted/re-defaulted. Tree clean.
**Probe (text):** POST /v1/chat/completions :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-**1059**`, coherent 270-char answer, **WALL 9.16s (OVER 5s target — known block-time-bound confirm-wait)**, exactly 1 claim/request (journal `claiming #1059` 12:23:26 → `done`). AP id 1059 == chain id (desync fix holding).
**NEW ANOMALY — stranded #1058 (concrete evidence):** on-chain `ComputeMarketplace::Jobs[1058]` present, `state=Pending`, `JobEscrow[1058]=512000000000` (≈**0.512 GPN LOCKED**), `JobRequestor[1058]=0xa8d73…c05079` (the AP account), `JobProvider[1058]=null`, `JobBilling[1058].actual_tokens=0`. BUT `Jobs[1058]` is **ABSENT from `~/.gpn/assignments.json` (AP store)** and **ABSENT from `~/.gpn/llm_store.json` (provider store)** — i.e. an on-chain job with **no client assignment and no provider processing**. Corroborated by journal: `#1057` claimed **twice** (12:19:37 **and** 12:19:44, two inferences) while `#1058` is never claimed. `NextJobId=1060` (best #84339); AP `/jobs` total 1059 {Completed 803, Cancelled 243, Failed 1, **Pending 12**} = documented baseline {305,306,345,568,656,683,684,685,687,709}+#988 **PLUS new #1058**. Stable >15 min (re-checked t+~10m and t+~16m: still Pending) ⇒ **not self-healing** (provider reconcile can only heal ids it has an off-chain `kind=llm,status=done` entry for — #1058 has none ⇒ never healed).
**WHY (root cause, from 0c5c6bb diff):** the AP confirm loop labels the job `real = after - 1` where `after = chain NextJobId` and `next_before` was read *before* the create. Under TWO concurrent creates (two crons probing ~7s apart), request B reads `next_before=1057`; request A's create lands first → B's first poll sees `after=1058>1057` and **attributes A's advance to itself**, labelling B as **1057** (already A's), `reseed_alloc(1058)`, and B's own on-chain job (**1058**) is left with **no label/assignment** (`assignments[1057]` overwritten). Result = on-chain escrow-locked orphan #1058 + double-processed #1057. That is exactly the observed shape (2 claims of 1057, 0 of 1058, store missing 1058). This is a *sibling-attribution* hole in the new confirm logic (the old code's `id==predicted` guard was removed by 0c5c6bb); it is **not** a lost/phantom create (the create *did* land).
**FIX: none applied this run** — correct fix lives in the APPROVED fast path (`gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_scheduler.rs` confirm block) ⇒ needs MAIN APPROVE + rebuild + AP restart; `sessions_list` shows only this cron ⇒ **no MAIN reviewer reachable**. No restart (no hang/deadlock), no state wipe, no workaround.
**PROPOSAL (WHAT/WHY/RISK/ROLLBACK — small + reversible, needs review+build):** WHAT: stop inferring our id as `after-1`; identify OUR created job explicitly — after create, scan `NextJobId` from `next_before..after` for the id whose `JobRequestor==AP` and `workload_hash==ours`, label with that; if none matches within the bound, mark FAILED (do NOT fall back to `predicted`) so we never mislabel a sibling. Serialize creates per-AP (mutex) as belt-and-braces. WHY: closes the sibling-attribution hole; ends escrow-locked orphans + duplicate processing. RISK: low — AP-only, read-only extra confirm; no chain/genesis/wasm change. ROLLBACK: `git revert <commit>`; env flags untouched.
**Escalate:** to JEREMY (approval authority) — this is a **funds-relevant** correctness bug (locked escrow) and also the concrete driver for open item **(8)/q8 pallet `expire_job`** (a permissionless timeout is the only recovery for already-stranded ids like #1058). Root cause is decided; only approval authority is missing.
**Blocked/open (unchanged):** (a) `submit_fast` retry-once/nonce-refresh; (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8); (c) RPC-readiness gate; (e) diffusion silent-wedge instrumentation; (f) item 6 Windows; (g) mainnet parity; (h) create-confirm latency vs <5s target. STATUS: CORE GREEN, DESYNC FIXED, **NEW: stranded #1058 + concurrent-create misattribution / NOT DONE**.

**EVIDENCE / ROOT CAUSE (concrete):** independently confirmed #1003 is **NOT on chain**: raw `ComputeMarketplace::NextJobId` = **1003** (jobs 0..1002 only; raw `0xeb03000000000000`), AP `GET /job/1003` → `{"error":"not found"}`, AP `GET /jobs` max id **1002** (total 1002, {Completed 747, Cancelled 243, Failed 1, **Pending 11**} = documented baseline {305,306,345,568,656,683,684,685,687,709} + the 09:08 orphan **#988**). `author_pendingExtrinsics` = **[]** (no create lingering) and **>10 min elapsed with blocks still advancing** ⇒ the create tx was **lost, not delayed**. Yet AP `~/.gpn/assignments.json` contains key **1003**, and the provider ran it from that assignment (`gpn_llm_provider` auto-claims AP-assigned ids via `read_assigned` → `assignments.json`, NOT only chain events) — so a user-visible 200 was served with **no on-chain job**.
Mechanism (from `gpn-agent/src/chain/mod.rs`): AP-account txs (create_job + janitor `cancel_job_fast` + completes) share a process-wide inclusion-free `NonceStream` (`submit_fast`). `submit_fast` returns **Ok as soon as the tx is accepted into the pool** (no inclusion wait); if a later submit re-seeds/reuses that same nonce (stream teardown on any Err → re-seed from chain, or a higher-priority AP tx for the same nonce), the create_job tx is **replaced/evicted before inclusion** and AP never learns — it proceeds with `(true, predicted)` and writes the assignment. Direct precedent in AP journal: `[janitor] cancel #711 failed: ServerError(1014) "Priority is too low: (2424 vs 2362)"` = an AP-account same-nonce replacement conflict. Consequence: AP's process-local `alloc_predicted_job_id` counter is now **ahead of the chain by 1** (local=1004, chain_next=1003) and never auto-resyncs down ⇒ every later create risks id-desync/orphan (open item (c)).
**WHY it matters:** user-facing success + provider work with no on-chain record (unsettled), and a standing AP id-desync that can cascade into further orphans. Distinct from the 09:08 #988 case (#988: create *landed* Pending, confirm wrongly rejected → error returned; **#1003: create accepted-but-never-included, no error returned → silent**). The 09:08 proposal (direct-lookup confirm in the **Err** branch) does NOT cover this **Ok** branch.
**FIX: none applied this run** (ROOT-CAUSE-FIRST + REVIEW-PROTOCOL: code change needs MAIN APPROVE). **PROPOSAL (small, reversible, needs review+build):**
- **WHAT:** (1) In `submit_fast` / `create_job_fast`, after an Ok (pool-accepted) create, **verify inclusion** of the predicted id (`client.get_job_state(predicted)` within a bounded ~1-block window); if absent, **tear down the nonce stream and retry once** with a fresh nonce before declaring success. (2) On any Ok-but-unconfirmed or allocation-mismatch, **re-seed the local allocator from chain** (`NEXT_JOB_ID = chain_next-1` before next alloc) so the AP id can't stay permanently ahead. (3) Do NOT write the assignment / return success until inclusion is confirmed.
- **WHY:** closes the silent-create hole on the Ok branch and stops the persistent off-by-one desync; keeps the fast path (no full-block wait) — confirmation is bounded and only on the create path.
- **RISK:** low — AP-only, read-only confirmation (+1 retry on the rare lost tx); no chain/genesis/wasm/tokenomics change.
- **ROLLBACK:** `git revert <commit>`; env flags untouched.
**Open (unchanged + this now evidence-backed):** (a) fast-AP create/submit_fast **retry-once + nonce-refresh + inclusion check** (THIS run's phantom #1003; the 09:08 #988; #709/#719/#720); (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8) + #711 race; (c) fast-AP id-desync allocator re-seed; (d) explorer subxt metadata-refresh; (d2) GET /jobs O(n) sequential RPC (~15s); (e) item 6 Windows; (f) mainnet parity. STATUS: CORE GREEN but **1 NEW phantom (lost create) this run** / NOT DONE.

Exact mechanism (from `gpn-agent/src/bin/gpn_scheduler.rs` fast-AP branch ~L626–665 + `chain/mod.rs submit_fast` L871): `next_before = get_next_job_id()` (still **987**, #987's fast create had not yet incremented the chain counter). `predicted = alloc_predicted_job_id(987)` → **988** (local allocator). `create_job_fast` → `submit_fast` returns **Err** here (pool/priority), so the code enters its bounded **2 s confirm loop**, polls `get_next_job_id()`: it observes `after = 988` (only **#987's** increment) → `real = after-1 = 987` → `987 != predicted 988` → the anti-sibling-race guard `id == predicted` **wrongly rejects the confirmation** → `(false, predicted)` → AP returns `[gpn error] could not create job`. But **#988's own extrinsic landed just after** the loop (node block 81907 @09:02:58 `extrinsics_count: 5`), so the chain got job #988 — which AP now believes failed, so AP **never wrote the assignment** → provider never claims → **orphan Pending**. i.e. a **false-negative create confirmation under back-to-back fast submits**: the confirm compares against a counter delta (`after-1`) instead of looking up the job AP actually created (`predicted`).
**WHY:** user-facing OpenAI-compatible calls can return `could not create job` for a job that in fact landed, and each occurrence leaks a Pending job toward the global `MaxActiveJobs` cap (same failure class as documented open items (a)/(c) — now reproduced with exact evidence: `chatcmpl-gpn-988`).
**FIX: none applied this run** (per ROOT-CAUSE-FIRST + REVIEW-PROTOCOL: a code change requires MAIN APPROVE; brute-forcing is disallowed). **PROPOSAL (small, reversible, needs review+build):**
- **WHAT:** in `gpn_scheduler.rs` fast-AP confirm loop, confirm by **directly looking up the predicted id on-chain** (`client.get_job_state(predicted)` exists → `(true, predicted)`) instead of requiring `after-1 == predicted`; keep the 2 s bound.
- **WHY:** `predicted` is uniquely allocated to this request by the process-local allocator (`alloc_predicted_job_id`, never behind the chain counter), so a direct existence check is correct and avoids the off-by-one caused by a sibling's increment being observed in the counter delta.
- **RISK:** low — AP-only, read-only confirmation; no chain/genesis/wasm change; worst case falls back to today's behaviour.
- **ROLLBACK:** `git revert <commit>` (env flags untouched), rebuild `gpn_scheduler`.
**Open (unchanged, + this now evidence-backed):** (a) fast-AP create-confirm / `submit_fast` retry-once+nonce-refresh (THIS run's #988, plus #709/#719/#720); (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8) + #711 race; (c) fast-AP id-desync allocator re-seed; (d) explorer subxt metadata-refresh; (d2) GET /jobs O(n) sequential RPC (~15s); (e) item 6 Windows; (f) mainnet parity. ## 2026-10-06 13:12 PT — monitor run: **FIXED (backend down → restarted Ollama)**; GREEN after fix
**ROOT CAUSE (evidence-first):** e2e probe via AP :8092 returned `[gpn error] error sending request for url (http://127.0.0.1:11434/v1/chat/completions)` + provider log `pull llama3.2:1b failed: error sending request ...11434/api/pull`, retry-looping on job #673 (~13:09→13:10:43). Evidence: **nothing listening on :11434** (`ss` empty), no `ollama` process, `~/.gpn/ollama-serve.log` frozen at **13:03:18.659** with NO shutdown/crash line (abrupt kill). ## 2026-10-06 12:09 PT — monitor run: INCIDENT FOUND + FIXED (AP chain-metadata desync after spec-8 node rebuild)
**Root-cause-first (evidence):** At **12:03:18** the node binary `gpn-node/target/release/gpn-node` was **rebuilt** and the whole fleet restarted; on-chain runtime `state_getRuntimeVersion` is now **specVersion 8** (was 7 at 11:41 — `expire_job`/item 8 landed). The `gpn-accesspoint` came up **~1s *before* the node** (AP 12:03:18 / node 12:03:19) and its subxt client fetched chain metadata **once at startup** → it cached **pre-upgrade (spec-7) metadata**. Symptom: `signer::connect_robust` fetched the old metadata (no error logged), so all AP **chain reads** silently failed — `GET /jobs` → `{"jobs":[]}` and `GET /job/{id}` → `"not found"` for every id (verified /job/649 & /job/655), while **POST /jobs still wrote on-chain** (create path doesn't need the metadata decode). Consequence: the AP allocated **predicted ids from 0** (`chain_next`=None→0) so my 2 probes created real on-chain jobs but were labelled #0/#1 off-chain → id desync → **2 NEW Pending orphans #655/#656** (requestor = AP hot wallet `0xa8d73283…`), invisible to the monitor's `/jobs` check.
**Fix (ONE, targeted):** restarted **only `gpn-accesspoint`** (not the provider/node) so it re-fetched spec-8 metadata from the now-stable node. Justified by root cause (stale startup metadata), not a reflex.
**Result / verify:** `/jobs` restored → **total 656 {Completed 427, Cancelled 222, Failed 1, Pending 6}**; `/job/655` now returns the real job (state Pending, requestor=hot wallet). Fresh probe via AP :8092 /v1/chat/completions (llama3.2:1b) → HTTP 200 coherent answer, **WALL 1.29s warm** (<5s MET); provider `claiming LLM job **#657**` == AP id (desync gone), 3 claims in the window (no storm). All 8 gpn units running; `GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` + `GPN_FAST_AP=1` intact.
**Remaining:** Pending = pre-existing {305,306,345,568} + NEW {655,656} from the 2 broken-window probes; the AP's in-memory janitor can't reclaim them (set cleared at restart, and they were tracked under predicted ids 0/1) — spec-8 `expire_job` (`eeb3cdd`, now live) is the designed permissionless remedy; **escalating to Jeremy**. Note: on-chain runtime is spec-8 but repo `gpn-node/runtime/src/lib.rs` still says `spec_version: 6` → deployed runtime does not match this tree.

## 2026-10-06 06:30 PT — monitor run: ROOT-CAUSED id-desync in fast-AP path (new orphan #568). No code deploy this run (needs MAIN review + build).

**Symptom observed:** this run's probe (POST /v1/chat/completions) returned HTTP 200 but body `[gpn error] could not create job` (2.07s). An immediate single retry succeeded (real 425-char answer). `/jobs` then showed a NEW on-chain Pending orphan **#568** (requestor 0xa8d7… = AP hot wallet, provider null).

**Evidence (root cause, not a guess):**
- The AP's fast path predicts the new job id from a **process-local** counter `NEXT_JOB_ID` (`alloc_predicted_job_id`, gpn_scheduler.rs:106). That counter advances on **every** request *before* the create is attempted — even when `create_job_fast` returns `Err` and the confirm window (`get_next_job_id` for ≤2s) never observes the id land.
- Measured offset: after the 06:21 probe's create was dropped, the AP's predicted ids are permanently **+1 vs the chain**. Proof from this run: AP predicted ids **570/571** for two concurrent probes while the chain assigned **569/570** (`/jobs` max 570 ⇒ chain `NextJobId`=571). Earlier: AP predicted 569 → chain created 568 (AP 569 = chain 568). Before 06:21 the two matched (566/567 = 566/567).
- Consequence: AP-keyed `llm_store.json` / `assignments.json` ids no longer match on-chain ids, so the on-chain job under the lower id is never matched by the provider/reconcile → stays **Pending** (orphan), and the caller intermittently gets `could not create job`.
- Not the cause: `MaxActiveJobs`=100 (only 4 Pending); tx pool empty; node/fleet mining normally; AP balance 1833.69 GPN (nonce 1204), provider capacity 4/4 free; no journal errors/panics; no hang.

**WHY no code change applied this run (WHAT/WHY/RISK/ROLLBACK — for MAIN review):**
- WHAT: on the fast-AP *unconfirmed* branch, re-seed the local allocator to the chain (`*NEXT_JOB_ID = get_next_job_id()`) so the next prediction realigns, and call `record_created(predicted,512)` so the janitor can reap a late-landing job.
- WHY: fixes the root cause (permanent +N desync after any dropped create) instead of the symptom.
- RISK: touches the **APPROVED/ON** fast path (changing logic, not defaults); under genuine concurrency a backward re-seed could momentarily collide with an in-flight prediction.
- ROLLBACK: revert the small diff; fast path otherwise unchanged.
- Deploy needs a clean `cargo build --release` of `gpn-agent` (no `target/` here ⇒ full build) + `gpn-accesspoint` restart — heavier/riskier than a monitor should do unreviewed.
- **Explicitly NOT done:** no service restart (no hang/deadlock; a restart would only be a workaround AND would drop the janitor's in-memory tracked-Pending set → more orphans).

**Result this run:** services active (AP PID 1110762, provider PID 1098961 ~16.5h); node mining (fleet head ~62989); tx pool drained; ~1 claim/request (5 claims/15min, no storm); e2e (retry) 6.08s — **over the <5s target**; residual Pending = {305,306,345 (pre-existing) + **568 (new, from this desync)**}. Escalating to Jeremy/MAIN for the reviewed fix; the desync will keep leaking ~1 on-chain Pending per probe until the allocator is realigned.

## 2026-10-06 13:54 PT — monitor run: FIXED (AP/provider chain clients stranded by the upgrade restart)
**Trigger (root-cause-first):** on-chain runtime was upgraded **spec 8 → 9** out-of-band ~13:45 (coinbase/block-producer reward; genesis `0xb8e4636a…` + canonical WASM `d9d2b536…` **unchanged** ⇒ forkless, no chain reset). During the upgrade the node self-shut down **twice** ("Essential task `txpool-background` failed", 13:45:03 + 13:46:05) → systemd `Restart=always` restarted **all** gpn units at the **same instant**.
**Cause (proved):** `gpn-accesspoint`+`gpn-provider` came up at 13:46:05, but the node's JSON-RPC server only bound at 13:46:06 → their subxt `OnlineClient` initialized before RPC was ready and **never recovered** (no retry). Symptom: AP `/jobs` → `{"jobs":[]}`, every `/job/{id}` → `not found` (incl. on-chain-existing #305), and `get_next_job_id`→**0** (real on-chain `NextJobId`=685) — i.e. the AP could neither READ nor CREATE on-chain jobs; e2e was served purely off-chain (probe ids `chatcmpl-gpn-0/1/2`, chain `NextJobId` did **not** advance).
**Fix (one, targeted, reversible):** ordered restart of `gpn-accesspoint`+`gpn-provider` at 13:52:16 **after** the node was up + stable (node not touched).
**Verified:** AP reads chain again — `/job/305` = `Pending` (requestor `0xa8d7…`, 512 GPN escrow); `/jobs` total **685** {Completed 434, Cancelled 241, Failed 1, Pending 9}; id prediction correct (**686**); new jobs land on-chain; e2e probe #686 HTTP200 coherent **WALL 1.33s (<5s MET)**; node head advancing 68330→68338, `NRestarts=0`, 0 new `txpool-background` failures. Pending = baseline {305,306,345,568,655,656} + transient probes {683,684,685}. Fast flags intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1`).
**OBSERVATION / flag:** repo `runtime/src/lib.rs` declares `spec_version: 6` while the live chain is **9**, and the built WASM (13:43) is spec-6 ⇒ the spec-9 runtime was built/deployed from **outside** this tree. Repo↔chain version **drift** — a future rebuild+set_code from HEAD would be a **downgrade**. Needs reconciliation + MAIN/Jeremy ack. STATUS GREEN (recovered) / queue item (8) still open.

## 2026-10-06 13:58 PT — monitor run: FIXED (full-stack simultaneous restart re-stranded AP/provider chain clients)
**Trigger (root-cause-first):** at **13:55:15 ALL gpn user units were stopped+started simultaneously** (journal: `Stopping gpn-accesspoint/diffusion/explorer/faucet/provider/fleet` then starts; node JSON-RPC only bound at **13:55:22**) — ~3 min after the 13:52:16 ordered fix. Actor not identifiable from available logs (no OpenClaw session other than this cron; NOT `topup-ap.sh` and NOT `run-fleet.sh` — neither restarts units). Looks like an external/manual `systemctl --user restart gpn.target`.
**Cause (proved):** AP/provider `subxt OnlineClient` initialised at 13:55:15, i.e. **before** node RPC bound at 13:55:22 → stranded, no retry (same failure mode as the 13:45 upgrade). Symptom: AP `/jobs` → `{"jobs":[]}` and `/job/305` → `not found` while on-chain state has 687 jobs and #305 = `Pending` (requestor `0xa8d7…`, 512 GPN escrow); AP create failed (`[gpn error] could not create job`); e2e survived only via GPN_FAST_AP off-chain path.
**Fix (one, targeted, reversible):** ordered `systemctl --user restart gpn-accesspoint gpn-provider` at **13:56:45**, after the node was up + stable (node untouched).
**Verified:** AP reads chain again — `/job/305`=`Pending`, `/jobs` total **687** {Completed 436, Cancelled 241, Failed 1, Pending 9}; e2e probe HTTP200 coherent **WALL 1.28s (<5s MET)** landed on-chain (id advanced → 688); exactly 1 claim/request, txpool `[]`, node head 0x10b0d→0x10b14 advancing, `NRestarts=0`. Pending = baseline {305,306,345,568,656} + restart-era transients {683,684,685,687} ⇒ **no new persistent orphan**. Fast flags intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` / `GPN_FAST_AP=1`).
**FLAG/RISK (needs Jeremy):** both the 13:45 upgrade and this 13:55:15 event restarted the **whole** gpn stack at once ⇒ AP/provider chain clients strand whenever they start before the node RPC is ready. Recommend a readiness gate (`ExecStartPre` wait-for-RPC, or `After=` the node unit) so a stack-wide restart cannot re-strand them — and identify what issued the 13:55:15 simultaneous restart. STATUS GREEN (recovered).

## 2026-10-06 14:01 PT / 21:01 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — VERIFY + ROOT-CAUSE; GREEN; e2e MET; no change applied (review channel down)
**Services (all healthy ⇒ no restart):** 9 gpn units loaded/active + `gpn-ap-topup.timer` waiting. `gpn-provider` PID 1321305 + `gpn-accesspoint` PID 1321304 active since 13:56:45, `NRestarts=0`. `journalctl --user -u gpn-provider`/`-u gpn-accesspoint` 20m = **0 errors/panics/fatal/deadlock/timeout**; jobs #688/#689 clean claim→done. `GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` (~/.gpn/provider.env) + `GPN_FAST_AP=1` (~/.gpn/accesspoint.env) intact. Node head `0x10b26` (~68390) advancing; txpool 5 pending (expected heartbeat/topup extrinsics). On-chain **specVersion=10** (`impl=gpn`). Canonical runtime WASM content md5 `d9d2b5369dcb11b42da466687c0f4077` **UNCHANGED** — but now **UNTRACKED in git** (see obs (i)).
**Probe:** POST /v1/chat/completions AP :8092 (llama3.2:1b, ~250-char forkless-upgrade/salted-commitment prompt) → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-689`, coherent 661-char answer, **WALL 1.87s (<5s MET)**. Exactly **1 claim/request** (#689 @13:59:25→done :26) = no storm, no id desync.
**Jobs:** AP GET /jobs total 688 {Completed 437, Cancelled 241, Failed 1, **Pending 9**} = baseline {305,306,345,568,656} + restart-era probes {683,684,685,687} ⇒ **no new persistent orphan**. `/job/305`=`Pending` (requestor 0xa8d7…, 512 GPN escrow) ⇒ AP chain-read OK.
**ROOT CAUSE (proven — recurring full-stack restart ⇒ AP/provider chain-client stranding):** (1) every app unit (`gpn-accesspoint/provider/diffusion/explorer/faucet`) declares **`Requires=gpn-fleet.service`**; systemd propagates stop to `Requires=` dependents, so a single fleet bounce stops+starts the whole stack in the same second (journal 13:45:03 / 13:46:05 / 13:55:15 = "Stopping gpn-fleet" + all apps). (2) At 13:55:15 fleet started but the **node JSON-RPC only bound at 13:55:22** (7 s later); AP/provider build their `subxt OnlineClient` at 13:55:15 with **no readiness retry** ⇒ stranded (AP `/jobs`=[], create fails, `get_next_job_id`=0) — identical to the 13:45 upgrade strand; e2e survived only via the off-chain `GPN_FAST_AP` path. (3) The "Essential task `txpool-background` failed" line is a **symptom** of the stop (always adjacent to systemd "Stopping gpn-fleet"), not the cause. The identity of the restart issuer remains unknown (no systemd audit logging) — needs `LogLevel=debug`/audit to pin, but the strand mechanism is independent of who issues the restart.
**PROPOSED FIX (one, targeted, reversible — NOT applied; needs MAIN APPROVE):** WHAT: add an RPC-readiness gate for the RPC-dependent units — `ExecStartPre=/home/jeremyg/.gpn/wait-for-rpc.sh` (poll `system_health` on :9944 until up, ≤30 s) in `gpn-accesspoint.service` + `gpn-provider.service`; optionally relax `Requires=`→`Wants=` for the apps so a node restart no longer bounces them. WHY: removes the init-before-RPC race = root cause above. RISK: low — unit-file only, no runtime/genesis. ROLLBACK: `git checkout` the unit files (or drop ExecStartPre) + `systemctl --user daemon-reload`. BLOCKED: MAIN reviewer UNREACHABLE (`sessions_list` = ONLY this cron, kind=other) ⇒ cannot self-approve per REVIEW-PROTOCOL; flagged to Jeremy.
**OBSERVATIONS (not acted on):** (i) commit `c1e199e` (mislabeled "docs(change-log)") bundled live source edits (`node/src/service.rs`, `pallets/gpu-pow/src/lib.rs`, `runtime/src/lib.rs` `spec_version 6→10`) **and deleted the canonical tracked runtime WASM**. Disk file md5 still equals the pre-deletion tracked blob (d9d2b536…) ⇒ **no corruption**, but the overwrite safeguard (tracked artifact) is gone. Recommend re-tracking `gpn-node/runtime/gpn_runtime.compact.compressed.wasm` (needs review). (ii) **spec drift persists:** on-chain specVersion **10**; HEAD `runtime/src/lib.rs` = **6** (9cbb89e reverted 10→6 as a side effect). A rebuild+set_code from HEAD would be a **downgrade** ⇒ reconcile before any deploy. (iii) Queue: (6) Windows rejoin NEEDS JEREMY; (8) `expire_job` runtime upgrade needs MAIN+Root; (9) mainnet future. Queue **blocked, not empty** ⇒ cron 95b27deb stays ENABLED.

## 2026-10-06 15:34 PT / 22:34 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 1a4f19ab) — FAULT FOUND (transient) → root cause proven; NO restart, NO code change applied (needs rebuild+restart ⇒ APPROVE)
**Trigger/root-cause (proven):** A **forkless on-chain runtime upgrade fired at 15:28:36** — node (`gpn-fleet`, NRestarts=0, PID 1320729 up since 13:55:15) logged `ComputeMarketplace declares internal migrations` + `gpn-marketplace: capacity migration pruned terminal jobs` **without restarting**; on-chain `state_getRuntimeVersion` went **spec 10 → 11** (commit `be390db` "feat(pow)…" bumped `runtime/src/lib.rs spec_version: 11`). The long-lived AP+provider `subxt OnlineClient`s were connected with **cached spec-10 metadata**, so their **first submit after the upgrade was rejected** by the tx-pool with `InvalidTransaction 1010 "Transaction has a bad signature"` (signature payload carries the stale spec_version). Both clients **self-heal** because `submit_fast` drops its `NonceStream` on Err (`*guard=None`) → the next submit reconnects w/ fresh metadata.
**Evidence:** (1) probe #709 create @15:34 failed fast (~2.1s request) → AP emitted `[gpn error] could not create job`. (2) probe #710 @15:36: create OK (reconnected), but provider's first pipelined claim **#710 @15:36:32 hit `ServerError(1010) "Transaction has a bad signature"`**, retry @15:36:34 succeeded → done :51; that extra reconnect round pushed e2e to **WALL 20.5s (>5s MISS, upgrade-transition artifact only)**. Only **1** such error in the whole provider journal today → one-shot, not recurring.
**NEW ORPHAN #709 (this run):** the AP's create tx **nonetheless landed on-chain** (#709 now exists: `state=Pending, provider=null, requestor=0xa8d7…)` but the AP believed it failed — its **2 s** create-confirmation poll in `gpn_scheduler.rs` is shorter than the ~10–12 s PoW block, so it never saw the id advance, recorded **no** `assignments.json` entry (704–708,710 present; **709 absent**), so the provider never claimed it ⇒ Pending orphan.
**Fix (targeted, NOT applied — needs rebuild+restart ⇒ MAIN APPROVE):** in `chain/signer.rs::submit_fast`, on Err do **one** immediate reconnect + retry of the same call (the stream drop already refreshes spec metadata) so the first post-upgrade request succeeds instead of surfacing an error/orphan; optionally raise the AP create-confirm window above one PoW block. Rejected blind alternatives: service restart (would drop the janitor in-memory set ⇒ more orphans; services are alive/self-healed) and any fast-path flag change (APPROVED-ON, must not revert).
**State now:** `gpn-provider`/`gpn-accesspoint`/`gpn-fleet` active, `NRestarts=0`; `/job/709` reachable ⇒ chain reads fine; txpool **0**; node head ~69585 advancing; `GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`+`GPN_FAST_AP=1` intact; AP :8092 `/health` {providers:1,ok}. 0 errors after the 15:36 self-heal.
**Blocked/open:** (a) apply retry-once+metadata-refresh fix above + re-track canonical WASM + reconcile spec drift (chain 11 vs tree) need MAIN APPROVE; (b) RPC-readiness-gate fix; (c) APPLY `expire_job` to baseline + new #709 orphan; (d) retire client-side janitor; (e) item (6) Windows rejoin NEEDS JEREMY; (f) mainnet parity (item 9). STATUS YELLOW→GREEN (transient, self-healed).

## 2026-10-06 15:50 PT / 22:50 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — FAULT FOUND: client-side janitor cancelled a job the provider had already served (#711) → work done but job Cancelled (provider unpaid, escrow refunded); cause = known job-lifecycle gap (queue item 8); NO restart, NO change applied (fix = runtime upgrade ⇒ needs MAIN APPROVE)
**Root-cause-first (evidence):** on-chain `GET /job/711` now reads **state=Cancelled**, yet `journalctl -u gpn-provider` shows the provider legitimately processed it: `claiming LLM job #711` 15:41:04 → `[job 711] done — 546 chars` 15:41:05. The AP janitor then claimed it: `[janitor] cancel #711 failed: ServerError(1014) "Priority is too low: (2424 vs 2362)"` 15:43:07, then `[janitor] cancelled stale Pending #711 (max_tokens 256, waited >120s)` 15:44:07. So the job sat **Pending** on-chain >120 s after the provider served it (its fast-path `accept_job` tx never landed = the documented "fast-path leak" that `process_once` heals via `Pending→Accepted→…→Completed`), the token-scaled janitor timeout (`stale_secs(256)=60+(256/20)*2=84→clamp 120s`) fired first, and the **cancel won the race against the provider's reconcile/heal** (#710/#712/#713 all healed cleanly; #711 did not). NET: real economic-correctness bug — provider did the inference, job Cancelled ⇒ provider unpaid, requester escrow refunded. This is exactly the client-side-janitor-vs-on-chain-expiry gap (changes-lost race) described in `shared_workspace/artifacts/project7/job-lifecycle-gap-analysis-2026-10-05.md`.
**Secondary (transient, self-healed):** the 15:43:07 janitor tx error (1014 priority-too-low) = one-shot tx-pool priority collision from the shared AP signer (`cancel_job_fast` same-nonce vs an in-pool tx); it retried next tick and succeeded — no orphan, not recurring.
**Fix (targeted, NOT applied — NOT a restart):** retire the client-side janitor in favour of a **pallet permissionless expire/timeout** (queue item 8; source/tests exist per 2026-10-05 note) so expiry is ordered on-chain and cannot race the provider's completion/reconcile; secondarily make the provider heal Pending-leaks more eagerly than the janitor's stale window. Rejected blind alternatives: service restart (would drop the janitor in-memory set ⇒ more orphans; services alive, `NRestarts=0`) and any fast-path flag change (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1`/`GPN_FAST_AP=1` APPROVED-ON, not reverted). Runtime change ⇒ **cannot apply without MAIN APPROVE; MAIN reviewer UNREACHABLE** (`sessions_list` = ONLY this cron).
**State now:** 9 gpn units active + `gpn-ap-topup.timer` waiting; `gpn-provider`/`gpn-accesspoint`/`gpn-fleet` active, `NRestarts=0`; node head `0x11094` (~69780) advancing; on-chain specVersion **11** == tree `spec_version: 11`; canonical WASM md5 `d9d2b5369dcb11b42da466687c0f4077` UNCHANGED + tracked (tree CLEAN); AP :8092 `/health` {providers:1,ok}.

**Root-cause-first (evidence, no blind fix):** probe#1 POST :8092 (llama3.2:1b, ~500-char prompt) returned HTTP200 body `[gpn error] could not create job` in **WALL 2.08s** — i.e. the AP's ~2 s create-confirm poll fired before the PoW tx could land, so the AP surfaced an error to the client; **verification: NO on-chain job exists for probe#1** (`/job/718` = a *different* client's diffusion job "a blue whale"/sd-turbo, price 10^13, provider 0x664c… = diffusion signer; `/job/{715,716,717,718,719}` confirms 718 is the image job). Did NOT restart (services alive; restart would drop the janitor in-memory set ⇒ more orphans) and did NOT touch the approved fast-path flags.
**Fix (targeted, NOT applied):** (a) raise the AP create-confirm window above one PoW block + (b) retry-once-with-metadata/nonce-refresh in `chain/signer.rs::submit_fast` so a create/claim that lands late still confirms instead of surfacing an error. ## 2026-10-07 10:53 PT / 17:53 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — ANOMALY (10th): phantom #1025 + PROVEN fast-AP id-desync (AP predicted 1026, chain job = 1025); root cause = pool-ack treated as landed create; no fix (needs MAIN APPROVE), no restart
**Root-cause-first (evidence gathered BEFORE any action):** unit state + provider journal + AP /jobs are healthy; the fault is in the fast-AP create path. No hang/deadlock in any log ⇒ **no restart** (restart drops the janitor in-memory set ⇒ more orphans). Fast-path flags APPROVED-ON and intact/live (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` provider.env + PID 1321305 environ; `GPN_FAST_AP=1` accesspoint.env + PID 1380823 environ); not reverted.
**Probe #1 (text):** POST /v1/chat/completions :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-1025`, coherent 297-char answer, **WALL 4.88s (<5s MET)**; provider claimed #1025 10:48:45 → done :48 (inference ran), 1 claim/request. BUT on-chain: `NextJobId=1025` (StorageValue 0x0401) stuck for **>4 min**, `Jobs[1025]` **ABSENT**, `author_pendingExtrinsics` **EMPTY**, blocks 83191-83220 all `extrinsics_count:1 / NoMoreTransactions`. ⇒ **phantom**: AP returned an id + ran inference, but NO on-chain create (same class as #1003-#1016/#1023/#1024). Not a cap/balance issue: `ActiveJobCount=11` (max 100), AP hot wallet `5Ft5q2AViqtBrnaZmUp86KeQDuwczo2KHze5MR99892hVd9B` free 1601.4 GPN (escrow = 0.512 GPN/job).
**DECISIVE drift proof — Probe #2 (immediately after):** POST :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-**1026**` "pong"; live pool transiently 3-5 txs, `NextJobId 1025→1026` at t≈12s. Now `Jobs[1025]` **FOUND**, `Jobs[1026]` **ABSENT** ⇒ the on-chain job created for probe #2 is id **1025**, while the AP labelled it **1026** and wrote assignments.json `1026`. **⇒ AP predicted id is off-by-one from the real on-chain id.**
**Actual root cause (code):** `gpn_scheduler.rs` fast path (`GPN_FAST_AP`) predicts the id via the process-local `alloc_predicted_job_id(next_before)=max(local,chain_next)` and submits via `create_job_fast`→`submit_fast`→`signer::NonceStream`→`submit_signed_no_wait`, which returns `Ok` on a **tx-pool ACK** and **deliberately ignores the dispatch outcome**. So when a `create_job` tx is dropped or fails at dispatch, the AP still sets `created=true` with the predicted id (phantom), and the local counter advances ⇒ allocator drifts +1 ahead of chain `NextJobId` ⇒ every later request's predicted id ≠ its real on-chain id. (The reconcile/self-heal covers accept/start/result/complete lanes, but there is NO reconcile for the create.)
**Services:** gpn-accesspoint PID 1380823 active (up 15h, NRestarts=0), gpn-provider PID 1321305 active (up 20h, NRestarts=0), gpn-fleet node best #83220 advancing, AP :8092 `/health` {providers:1, ok}, provider :8093 ok. journalctl provider fault-grep (panic/fatal/deadlock/error/1010/1014) last 25m = **0 hits**.
**Blocked/open (unchanged, need MAIN APPROVE/JEREMY; MAIN session not visible to this cron ⇒ unreachable):** (a) `submit_fast` retry-once/nonce-refresh + **AP create-confirm window** (verify `NextJobId` advanced before declaring `created`; else re-seed allocator from chain + retry once) — THIS is the fix for the phantom; (d) fast-AP id-desync allocator re-seed (make predicted id self-correct when it drifts). Touches the APPROVED fast path ⇒ cannot apply without approval. (b) janitor→pallet `expire_job` (q8); (c) RPC-readiness gate; (e) diffusion silent-wedge instrumentation; (f) item 6 Windows; (g) mainnet parity. STATUS: CORE GREEN, ANOMALY PRESENT / NOT DONE.

## 2026-10-07 14:45 PT / 21:45 UTC — GPN queue driver (item 1, step 2) — provider advertises `/status` endpoint ON-CHAIN; root-caused the "variant doesn't exist" txpool rejection; LIVE-VERIFIED
**Item 1 (on-chain provider discovery) — provider side DONE.** `gpn_llm_provider` now advertises `$GPN_PROVIDER_ENDPOINT` on-chain at startup via new `ChainClient::set_provider_endpoint` (commit `e463b4c`), called only when the env var is set (non-fatal on error).
**Root cause of the spec-12 call being rejected (`Could not decode RuntimeCall::ComputeMarketplace.0: variant doesn't exist`):** the running `gpn-node` binary was built **Oct-6 13:53**, BEFORE the pallet gained `set_provider_endpoint` (committed Oct-7 13:29). A Substrate node's **txpool decodes extrinsics with the node binary's compile-time `Call` enum**; forkless `set_code` replaces only the executor runtime, so a **new call variant is invisible to the txpool until the node binary is rebuilt** (hence `expire_job` works — the node was rebuilt after it). On-chain metadata already had call_index 10; the node just couldn't decode it.
**Fix (smallest correct):** rebuilt `gpn-node` (runtime `build.rs` embeds the committed canonical WASM via `include_bytes!`, so genesis is untouched) and restarted `gpn-fleet`. Canonical bundled `gpn-node/runtime/gpn_runtime.compact.compressed.wasm` md5 `d9d2b536…d4077` **UNCHANGED**; genesis still `0xb8e4636a…` (no reset); specVersion 12.
**Verify (live):** `ProviderEndpoints[5ENqSp5Q…] = http://gpn-testnet.duckdns.org:8093/status`; `ProviderEndpointSet` event in block **#85883** (who = 0x664c6e12… = the provider, `RegisteredProviders=true`). Restarted dependents (`gpn-accesspoint`/`gpn-diffusion`/`gpn-provider-heartbeat`/`gpn-provider`) per the post-upgrade rule. TEXT e2e via AP :8092 → HTTP 200 "ENDPOINT-OK" (#1102) **5.83s**; NextJobId advanced ⇒ create path healthy.
**Notes:** (a) heartbeat port **8093 is NOT publicly forwarded** (8092/9944 are) → remote-AP discovery needs it exposed; advertised URL uses the public duckdns path. (b) minor/cosmetic: `submit_signed` logged a FALSE `ProviderNotRegistered` for the call that actually succeeded (mis-attribution when `our_index` can't be resolved against the block) — on-chain effect was correct; follow-up.
**NEXT (item 1):** scheduler builds its registry from the chain (`RegisteredProviders`+`ProviderEndpoints`) instead of the static `--providers` list. **Commit `e463b4c`.** No runtime/genesis change this run.

## 2026-10-07 14:52–15:00 PT / 21:52–22:00 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 95b27deb) — post-**node-rebuild** provider settlement STALL (jobs Pending ~19 min, heal-loop churn) → **self-resolved**; NO action, NO change
**Root-cause-first (evidence before any action):** unit state + provider/AP/fleet journals + direct chain reads reviewed first. **No panic/fatal/deadlock/1010/1014** in provider/AP journal (fault-grep = only the benign startup `set_provider_endpoint → ProviderNotRegistered` mis-attribution line at 14:38:47) ⇒ **no restart** (no hang; reflex restarts forbidden). Fast-path flags APPROVED-ON and intact (`GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1` ~/.gpn/provider.env; `GPN_FAST_AP=1` ~/.gpn/accesspoint.env) — not reverted/re-defaulted.
**Anomaly observed (14:40→14:59, ~19 min):** the first jobs created after the 14:36 `gpn-node` rebuild + `gpn-fleet` restart — **#1102 (14:40:09), #1103, #1104** — landed on-chain as `Pending` (escrowable create OK) but their **accept/start/submit/complete never advanced the on-chain state** for ~19 min. Direct chain read (`ComputeMarketplace::Jobs`, twox128+Blake2_128Concat) showed **#1102/#1103 Pending** while the provider journal spammed `[provider] healed leaked Pending #1102 → Completed` every ~10–25 s (reconcile heal loop). Block production was healthy (best #86053→#86115) but the author's blocks carried **only the block inherent** (`chain_getBlock` extrinsics=**1**) and `author_pendingExtrinsics` churned **0↔~237** provider-signer txs — i.e. pipelined no-wait txs were repeatedly submitted but not included, then dropped.
**Root cause:** the provider signer's **`NonceStream` diverged from the chain** across the 14:36 **node rebuild / txpool recreation** (fleet log: `Essential task txpool-background failed. Shutting down service` → `Creating transaction pool` at 14:37:01). The `c4a4532` **fast path (`submit_signed_no_wait`) never confirms inclusion**, and `NonceStream::resync()` only pulls forward — so a batch of dropped-but-nonce-consumed txs left the local counter out of step with the chain, and the reconcile consequently re-healed the same `Pending` jobs in a no-op loop. This is the live manifestation of open items **(a) `submit_fast` retry-once + nonce reseed** and **(d) fast-AP allocator re-seed** — triggered by node maintenance (NOT a spec change this time; `specVersion` stayed **12**, so the `runtime_spec_version` reseed trigger did not fire). Same failure class as the 14:01 spec-12 stall, but the trigger was a **node rebuild**, not an upgrade.
**Resolution: NONE applied by this monitor.** The backlog **self-drained at ~14:59** (coincident with a fresh create #1105 that filled the nonce gap): direct chain read now shows **#1102 Completed, #1103 Completed, #1105 Completed, #1104 Cancelled**; the heal loop **stopped** at 14:59:55 (no entries since 15:00). No restart, no code change, no state wipe.
**Verification (this run):** services all **8/8 active** (ap/provider/fleet/diffusion/explorer/faucet/provider-heartbeat/fleet-directive); AP :8092 `/health` {providers:1,ok}; provider :8093 ok; node :9944 best #86115 advancing, spec 12; direct `NextJobId` **1106** (advanced). TEXT e2e via AP :8092 → HTTP200 `chatcmpl-gpn-1105` (model llama3.2:1b) **WALL 4.58 s (<5 s MET)**, warm repeat **1.53 s**; earlier cold probe 6.69 s (cold model warm-up). On-chain **#1105 Completed** ⇒ create→claim→complete path healthy again.
**Jobs:** AP `GET /jobs` total **1104 {Completed 842, Cancelled 246, Pending 15, Failed 1}**; non-terminal = documented stranded set **{1058,1068,1079,1094}** (provider=null) ⇒ **NO new persistent orphan** (1102/1103/1105 healed to Completed).
**Notes:** (i) the earlier 14:45 entry's "#1102 e2e ok ⇒ create path healthy" was optimistic — #1102 actually stayed Pending on-chain until 14:59. (ii) The durable fix remains item **(a)+(d)** (confirm-inclusion / nonce-reseed on the pipelined path); a node rebuild or node restart is enough to trigger this stall, so it will recur on the next maintenance until (a)/(d) land — needs MAIN APPROVE (approver unreachable: `sessions_list` = only this cron). STATUS: **CORE GREEN (recovered); fast-path nonce-divergence recovery gap OPEN (a)/(d).**

## 2026-10-07 15:03 PT / 22:03 UTC — item 1 DONE — scheduler now DISCOVERS providers ON-CHAIN (no runtime/genesis change)
**What:** `ChainClient::list_provider_endpoints()` (new) enumerates `ComputeMarketplace::ProviderEndpoints` (`(hex account, url)`), decoding the variable-length `Vec<u8>` endpoint via a new `bytes_from_dynamic` helper (fixes the old 32-byte-only account decoder). `gpn_scheduler::refresh` now builds its provider registry from the **union** of (a) the static `--providers` list (kept as a bootstrap fallback) and (b) **on-chain-discovered** endpoints, polling `/status` of each; entries resolving to the same provider address are de-duplicated (lowest-latency kept). Startup prints `on-chain discovery: ENABLED`. Off-chain agent binary only — **no `set_code`, no spec bump, no genesis change, canonical WASM untouched**; nothing to restore.
**Build/test:** `cargo build --release --bin gpn_scheduler` clean; new unit test `bytes_from_dynamic_reads_variable_length_vec_u8` green (`http://gpn-testnet.duckdns.org:8093/status` round-trips, not truncated to 32 B).
**Deploy + live-verify:** backed up old binary to `~/.local/bin/gpn_scheduler.bak-*`, copied the new one, `systemctl --user restart gpn-accesspoint` (running, PID 1569087, banner logged). `GET :8092/providers` now lists **both** `http://127.0.0.1:8093` (static/healthy, gflops 30000, 3 models) **and** `http://gpn-testnet.duckdns.org:8093/status` — the public URL is **only obtainable from the chain**, proving chain-read discovery works end-to-end.
**Carry-forward (infra, NOT code):** public probe shows `duckdns:8092/health` = 200 but `duckdns:8093/status` = 000 (connection refused) → port **8093 is not forwarded** (only 8092/9944 are), so an *external* access point still can't reach the advertised endpoint even though discovery itself works (its registry entry shows `healthy:false`). Fix = expose 8093 (router forward) or have the AP proxy provider `/status` on 8092. On the single-box testnet the static `127.0.0.1:8093` fallback keeps routing working.
**NEXT item now = #2 (off-chain escrow / payment channels — the high-priority one).**

## 2026-10-08 03:20 UTC / 2026-10-07 20:20 PT — GPN queue driver (item 5b, video gen) — **alias bug FIXED; item BLOCKED on hardware (T5-XXL)**
**Item 5b (exercise + verify video generation / ltx-video)** — first TODO after 2/4 BLOCKED, 1/3/5a DONE.
**Root-caused before acting:** ran the helper directly. Found a real bug: `gpn_diffuse.py::model_id()` resolved image aliases (`sd-turbo`…) but **not the video aliases**, so the default/`auto` video model passed the literal string `"ltx-video"` to `LTXPipeline.from_pretrained()` → guaranteed failure `OSError: Cannot load model ltx-video`. (The provider's `ensure_model` mapped it for *downloading*, but the helper that actually builds the pipeline did not.)
**Fix (commit `19536de`):** `model_id(m, kind)` now maps `ltx-video`/`ltx`/`auto`→`Lightricks/LTX-Video` (unit-checked). Added a memory-fitting load path in `gen_video`: on small GPUs (<10 GB total) auto-load an **NF4 4-bit T5 pinned to CPU** (`device_map={'':'cpu'}`) and move only the small transformer+VAE to the GPU, + VAE tiling/slicing. Big GPUs keep the exact prior unquantized path. Env override `GPN_DIFFUSE_T5_4BIT={auto,1,0}`. Installed `bitsandbytes 0.50.2` (PyPI; py3.12/cu124).
**Blocked — hardware (empirical):** LTX-Video's cost is its **T5-XXL** text encoder (~18 GB fp32 / ~22 GB fp16); the 2B transformer is only ~7 MB fp32 (7.2 GB). Two cgroup-isolated attempts (MemoryMax 8–11G, `--user --scope`, so the node was never at risk):
  - naive `.to("cuda")` → `CUDA OOM: tried to allocate 5.93 GiB, 4.47 GiB free` (node 1.09 GB + ollama 1.52 GB already hold VRAM);
  - 4-bit T5 `device_map=cpu` → scope **OOM-killed** after ~3 min (bnb must stage the fp32 shards, ≈18 GB, to quantize → exceeds this host's 15 GB RAM, ~5.4 GB free).
⇒ This host (RTX 4060 **8 GB VRAM / 15 GB RAM**) cannot render LTX-Video at any quantization; matches the 2026-09-03 note + USE-GPN §4.3. **No chain/genesis/runtime change. No restart.** Node untouched (verified: cgroup isolated, best # advancing). **Next (needs a bigger host):** render on ≥16 GB VRAM + ≥32 GB RAM, or add a pre-quantized (GGUF) T5 loader / a lighter video model. Docs updated (USE-GPN §4.3, GPN-TASKS 5b). **NOTE:** the helper has no `Command` timeout in the provider → a genuinely-hanging gen would wedge the diffusion lane (item 10 scope).

## 2026-10-07 20:18 PT / 03:18 UTC — PM07 GPN debug (cron 1a4f19ab) — **FAULT FOUND + FIXED: gpn-accesspoint chain client stranded (reads→0, creates mislabeled) after 20:12 RPC drop; ordered restart**
**Root-cause-first (evidence before action):** provider/AP/fleet journals + chain RPC (`NextJobId`, `Jobs`, `system_health`) + AP `/jobs` + live probes FIRST.
**Anomaly (this run):** probe POST :8092 → HTTP200 but body `[gpn error] could not create job` (one transient), then probes returned real answers **but with response ids `chatcmpl-gpn-0/1/2/3`** while on-chain **`NextJobId`=1193** (read directly via `state_getStorage`). Provider journal 20:12:24: `pipelined claim #1133 failed: Rpc error: … The background task closed connection closed; restart required` — the node's JSON-RPC WS dropped ~20:12 (node itself NOT restarted: `gpn-fleet` up 5h39m, best # advancing).
**Cause (proved):** subxt `OnlineClient` background task in **gpn-accesspoint** died on the 20:12 RPC disconnect and has **no reconnect**, so `get_next_job_id()`→0 (`storage_at_head`→None→`unwrap_or(0)`) and `get_job_state()`→None ⇒ `/jobs`=[], `/job/*`=not found, and the fast-AP allocator re-seeded at 0 → predicted ids 0..n. Writes still submitted (inclusion-free `submit_fast`), so on-chain jobs **1190–1193** were created but labeled 0–3 and never correlated ⇒ e2e survived only off-chain. Same class as the earlier documented strand (13:54/13:58), now triggered by a mid-run RPC drop rather than an init-before-RPC race.
**Fix (ONE, targeted, reversible):** `systemctl --user restart gpn-accesspoint gpn-provider` at 20:17:20 — **ordered after** confirming node RPC healthy (`gpn-fleet` active, `system_health` ok). Node NOT touched; no code/env/flag change; fast-path flags (GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1/GPN_FAST_AP=1, committed 9dcedb7) intact.
**Verified:** AP reads chain again — `/job/305`=`Pending` (requestor `0xa8d7…`, 512 GPN escrow); `GET /jobs` → HTTP200 total **1194** {Completed 899, Cancelled 275, Failed 1, **Pending 19**}; probe ids now correct `chatcmpl-gpn-1194/1195/1196` (prediction matches chain). e2e: cold first probe **5.80 s** → warm **3.63 s / 1.83 s (<5 s MET)**. Exactly 1 claim/request (#1194→1196), txpool `[]`, no storm.
**Residual (fault artifact, NOT deleted — deletion forbidden):** Pending now = documented baseline {305,306,345,568,656,683,684,685,687,709,988,1058,1068,1079,1094} **+ NEW {1190,1191,1192,1193}** — the 4 on-chain jobs the stranded AP created during 20:15–20:16 with mislabeled ids. They will not self-settle (their off-chain records are keyed 0–3); left in place per policy (on-chain `expire_job` needs the runtime upgrade/root). **ESCALATED to Jeremy** (also needs the still-open strand fix: RPC-readiness gate / auto-reconnect). STATUS: RED (AP chain-dead) → GREEN (recovered); 1 orphan artifact left.

## 2026-10-08 00:47 UTC — PM07 driver (cron d496e0b0) — item **#9b janitor → pallet `expire_job`** → **DONE (live-verified)**
**Root cause:** the AP stale-job janitor could only reclaim its **own still-`Pending`** jobs via requestor-side `cancel_job`; the pallet's **permissionless `expire_job`** (live since spec 7/8) was never called. So provider-abandoned `Accepted`/`Running` orphans, and orphans lost from the in-memory list across an AP restart, held a global `ActiveJobCount` slot forever.
**Fix (`bee8be0`, no runtime/genesis change):** `ChainClient::expire_job`/`expire_job_fast` (call_index 9; permissionless; any non-terminal state) + `get_job_timing(created_at,max_duration)`. The `gpn_scheduler` janitor now reclaims **primary** via the pallet `expire_job` for any non-terminal job past `created_at+max_duration` (also clears Accepted/Running orphans), keeps requestor `cancel_job` as the fast path for our own stale Pending jobs, and **seeds tracking from the last 256 chain job ids at startup** so a restart no longer leaks slots. Pure helpers `is_terminal_state`/`past_deadline` + 2 unit tests.
**Build/tests:** `cargo test --bin gpn_scheduler` **6/6**; `cargo test --lib` **68/68**; `cargo build --release --bin gpn_scheduler` clean.
**Live:** stopped → swapped `~/.local/bin/gpn_scheduler` → started `gpn-accesspoint`; log `[janitor] re-adopted 8 non-terminal job(s) from #1044..#1299` then all 8 **expired on-chain** via `ComputeMarketplace::expire_job` (`block 93025 > deadline …`). Fresh e2e create `#1300` (llama3.2:1b via AP :8092) → Completed. **On-chain `expire_job` confirmed working** (permissionless, from the AP account). Canonical genesis WASM untouched.
**Next:** queue's next TODO is **#9c (RPC-readiness gate)**. Items 2/6/7 spec-13 `set_code` remain gated on explicit go-ahead.

**Root-cause-first (evidence before action):** all 8 gpn units active (AP PID 1715155 up since 00:47, node PID 1563519 authoring: best block 93815→93848 advancing, on-chain `NextJobId`=1344 read via `state_getStorage`). Probes FIRST, no restart.

Account `Failed`=9. **No panics/warnings** in AP or provider journals since 00:47.
**Anomaly (observed, reproduced):** `GET /jobs` sometimes returns a **complete but truncated** list — valid JSON ending at id **1233** and **1262** on two fetches, while three later full fetches and 3 python chunked reads all returned the full **1343** (bytes ~63.6 KB, chunked, consistent ~21 s). Single listener on :8092 (one `gpn_scheduler`) ⇒ not a second process.
**Cause (characterized):** the `GET /jobs` handler (`gpn_scheduler.rs:1132`) loops `for id in 0..next { if let Some(..) = get_job_state(id) { push } }`; `get_job_state`→`storage_at_head` swallows any transient RPC error to `None` (`.ok()?`). A brief read/connection hiccup at id N makes N..drop out ⇒ the listing silently under-reports (never errors, never logs). Read-only; **no impact on creates** (ids stayed correctly correlated with chain `NextJobId`) or settlement. Distinct from the 20:18 strand (which mislabeled creates — not seen here) and from `at_latest` fallback (finalized head is genesis ⇒ fallback would yield 0, not 1262).
**Action: NONE** — no hang/deadlock (no restart), no code/env/flag change (a fix = retry-or-surface the None read in the `/jobs` loop, a change to the AP read path ⇒ needs MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE; `sessions_list` shows only this cron ⇒ MAIN unreachable mid-run). Fast-path flags (GPN_FAST_CLAIM=1/GPN_FAST_AP=1) intact. **STATUS: GREEN** (core); one minor open read-robustness candidate for REVIEW. Not escalated to Jeremy (non-functional, self-recovering).

## 2026-10-08 17:15 PT / 00:15 UTC (+1) — PM07 GPN queue-driver (cron d496e0b0) — item #14: Windows box now CLAIMS (deploy+start verified live); completion root-caused to a prompt-delivery gap in the AP↔remote-provider path
**WHAT:** Deployed the `31dc849` Windows bundle to the box and restarted its provider — `windows-provider-deploy.yml` (run `37862554157`, inputs `run_id=37856682989`) then `windows-provider-start.yml` (run `37862631897`), both **success** (self-hosted runner `DESKTOP-USBUADL` online). Then live-tested routing/claim/completion.
**WHY:** item #14's documented next step after the `31dc849` claim-filter fix was "deploy to the box → confirm a `min_vram_mb:16000` job claims **and completes** on the 5070 Ti".
**RESULT (verified live):** box now **claims** remote-assigned jobs (previously it never claimed): `POST :8092/jobs {min_vram_mb:16000, model:qwen2.5:7b}` → **#1755 routed_to `5FHneW46…` → Claimed → `Running`**; second floored job **#1756** likewise Claimed→Running; a *local* 4060 job **#1757 → Completed** (no regression). So the `31dc849` fix is confirmed live.
**ROOT CAUSE of the remaining gap (completion):** a REMOTE provider can claim but never *run/complete*. The prompt is written only into the **AP host's** `~/.gpn` LlmStore (`gpn_scheduler.rs`: `LlmStore::put(id, LlmJob::new_llm(...))` then waits on *its own* store for status `done`). The box has a **different** store (`HOME` unset ⇒ `System32\.gpn\`), and `dispatch_assignment` / `POST /assign` (served by `gpn_provider_status`) deliver only `{job_id,model,pull}` — **no prompt**. Hence the box's `run_job` has no local `get_job(id)` and both the fast-path and the main `Running` scan skip it (`if let Some(job) = get_job(id)`) ⇒ it sits `Running` until the item-9e expiry sweep reclaims it (no permanent leak).
**RISK:** low for what was done (artifact deploy + service restart, no genesis/runtime/`set_code`; canonical genesis WASM `76f37b23…` untouched). The remaining fix is NOT applied.
**ROLLBACK:** box provider binary — redeploy a prior `release-windows` run-id via `windows-provider-deploy`.
**NEXT (needs MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE — touches the AP request path):** include the prompt in the assignment and have the provider persist it into its own local LlmStore on `/assign`, and add a result-return channel back to the requesting AP. Off-chain only; no genesis/runtime change.
**No code commit this run** (diagnostics + GH-Actions dispatches + docs/log only).

The `*_fast` variants return `Ok` on **txpool submission, not inclusion**, so on the next ~11 s poll the state is *still* `Pending` and it heals **again** — emitting **duplicate accept/start/result/complete extrinsics** each tick until the first set lands. Benign: every job settled, no orphan.
**Orphans/claims:** `GET /jobs` total 1818 {Completed 1505, Cancelled 288, Failed 20, **Pending 1 (#1818, just-created mid-settle)**, Running 4} ⇒ **no Pending orphan spike**; 21 claims/60 min (~1 claim/request). The 4 `Running` = `#1755/#1756` (known pre-e773823 *remote* Windows-box jobs, store `queued`) + `#1778`/`#1805` (local signer `0x664c6e12…`; **verified NO `~/.gpn/llm_store.json` payload entry** ⇒ the known prompt-delivery wedge; item-9e janitor `expire_job` is the reclaim path). Count unchanged from the 20:41 PT run; peers `#1818` = `done`.
**Action taken: NONE** (no hang/deadlock ⇒ no restart; a real fix — debounce/guard the reconcile loop so it doesn't re-submit `*_fast` for a job it healed in the last N blocks (e.g. track an in-flight/pending-heal set, or have the `*_fast` helper wait for inclusion) — **touches the APPROVED fast path ⇒ needs MAIN REVIEW/APPROVE**; no state wipe/workaround). ## 2026-10-08 21:35 PT / 04:35 UTC — PM07 GPN queue-driver (cron d496e0b0), item #16 red-team — FINDING #7 (agent/AP auth bypass) FIXED + LIVE-VERIFIED (`f0a8c55`, off-chain; no genesis/runtime)
**Finding #7:** the provider heartbeat `POST /assign` on the publicly-forwarded :8093 was **unauthenticated** — any remote caller could inject an arbitrary `LlmJob` into the provider's own store (unauthorized GPU work, no on-chain escrow) and **overwrite a pending assignment's `callback`/`callback_token`**, so the provider would POST a **user's** inference result to the attacker (**result hijack / data exfiltration**). Reproduced live: `POST /assign` → 200 with no token (local + box :8093 reachable).
**Fix:** shared AP↔provider secret. `gpn_provider_status`: new `--assign-token`/`GPN_ASSIGN_TOKEN`; `POST /assign` rejects a **non-loopback** caller whose `X-Gpn-Assign-Token` doesn't match (constant-time → 401); loopback exempt; unset token = legacy-accept + warn (non-breaking rollout). `gpn_scheduler::dispatch_assignment` sends the token (header + body). `windows-provider-start.yml` passes the repo secret to the box heartbeat. +4 tests.
**Verify (non-loopback = host LAN IP):** no token → 401, wrong → 401, correct → 200; loopback no-token → 200; e2e creates **#1820/#1821 Completed** (no regression). Token set in `~/.gpn/accesspoint.env` + `gpn-provider-heartbeat.service`; bins reinstalled; services restarted.
**Carry-forward:** set the `GPN_ASSIGN_TOKEN` GitHub secret (= AP host token) then `windows-provider-deploy`+`start` so the box enforces it (box `.exe` predates the fix → legacy until redeployed). STATUS: GREEN.
